● Stage D delivered — aggregation pushdown via facet bitmaps,
  correctness-verified against the normal plan.

  What landed

  - New module src/aggregate.rs: SfstFacetRule (OptimizerRule, BottomUp) →
  Extension(SfstFacetNode) → SfstExtensionPlanner/SfstQueryPlanner →
  SfstFacetExec calling IndexReader::facets().
  - Entry point sfst_datafusion::session_context() — wires the rule + planner
  (the CLI now uses it, so GROUP BY demos hit the bitmaps). Plain
  SessionContext::new() still works via the normal plan.
  - ColumnSpec gained tier (plan-time eligibility); CLI switched to the pushed
  context.

  Correctness (the hard part)

  Pushed result is identical to the normal aggregation plan, validated directly
  (facet_pushdown_matches_normal_plan), including the subtle NULL group (rows
  lacking the field = matched_count − Σ facet counts). It falls back — verified
  by EXPLAIN — whenever it can't guarantee correctness: high-card/list group
  column, non-COUNT(*) aggregate, untranslatable WHERE, or a WHERE that
  constrains the group column (facets exclude their own selection).

  Performance

  GROUP BY low-card field: 64.7ms → 21.6ms (~3×), results identical. Honest
  framing: facets only apply to low/mid-card fields (high-card can't facet),
  which are already cheap to aggregate normally — so the absolute win is modest
  here. The advantage is big-O (O(distinct values) vs O(rows)), so it widens
  with row count.

  No format change, no new sfst API

  facets() + matched_count() sufficed; the per-chunk KvId-range fact you raised
  was already in META, and tier came from the existing field_table. Your freeze
  holds.

  State

  - clippy clean; 84 sfst lib + 10 integration + doctest pass.
  - Uncommitted — the prior commit cc25bffee7 predates Stage D. Changed since:
  aggregate.rs (new), lib.rs, schema.rs, table.rs, examples/sql.rs,
  tests/query.rs.

  Follow-ups (tracked, not done): date_bin/timeline GROUP BY pushdown (2-D
  time×value grid via IndexReader::timeline), multi-field GROUP BY.

  Want me to commit Stage D? And is there a next direction, or is this a good
  stopping point?

● Committed Stage D as 4a25fe23ef (6 files, no AI attribution). Excluded the two
  SOWs + run-ingest-v8.sh as before.

  The follow-ups explained

  These are the two GROUP BY shapes Stage D doesn't accelerate yet (they
  currently fall back to the correct-but-normal plan).

  1. date_bin/timeline — grouping by time.
  Stage D pushes grouping by a value field (GROUP BY severity). This follow-up
  is grouping by a time bucket — the classic "logs over time" query:
  - SELECT date_bin('1 hour', timestamp), COUNT(*) FROM logs GROUP BY 1 — count
  per hour.
  - SELECT date_bin('1 hour', timestamp), severity, COUNT(*) … GROUP BY 1, 2 — a
  2-D time × value grid (e.g. log volume per hour, split by severity — a
  stacked time series).
  - SFST already has the exact primitive: IndexReader::timeline(field, filter, 
  grid), which computes that 2-D grid instantly from the bitmaps + the timestamp
  index.
  - Why not done: the rule must recognize DataFusion's date_bin(...) scalar
  function in the GROUP BY, translate its interval/origin into SFST's Grid
  (start/width/num_buckets), handle the 2-D case, and map the Timeline result
  back. Wiring + arg-translation work; the engine side exists.
  - Value: high — this is the bread-and-butter observability query
  (histograms/dashboards). It's the time-axis complement to Stage D's
  value-axis.

  2. Multi-field GROUP BY — grouping by two+ fields.
  - SELECT a, b, COUNT(*) FROM logs GROUP BY a, b — counts per distinct (a, b)
  pair.
  - Why not done: facets() is single-field by design (per-value counts for one
  field). The cross-product of (a,b) pairs isn't something the facet bitmaps
  give directly — you'd have to intersect each value-pair's bitmaps, which can
  combinatorially explode.
  - Value: lower, and it's a poorer fit for the index. This one is realistically
  "stays a correct fallback" rather than something worth pushing down — I
  tracked it mainly as a known boundary, not a clear TODO.

  My take: if you continue Stage D, the date_bin/timeline pushdown is the one 
  worth doing — real query, big-O win at scale, and the SFST primitive is
  already there. Multi-field GROUP BY I'd leave as fallback unless a concrete
  need shows up.

  Want me to build the timeline pushdown, or stop here?

● Critical facts confirmed: date_bin(interval, ts, origin) uses interval type
  IntervalMonthDayNano, default origin = epoch (0 ns), and bins as origin + 
  floor((source-origin)/stride)*stride. So I must reject month-based intervals
  (variable width) and align my Grid to origin + k*stride. My pushed-vs-normal
  test will catch any boundary mismatch.

  For the 1-D case (GROUP BY date_bin(ts) with no value field), timeline() needs
  a field — so I'll add a clean timeline_totals primitive to sfst (you invited
  extending it). Let me find where to add it.

      214 +/// Largest grid this rule will build; beyond it (e.g. 1-secon
          +d buckets over a                                              
      215 +/// year) we fall back rather than allocate an enormous bucket
          + vector.                                                      
      216 +const MAX_TIMELINE_BUCKETS: usize = 1_000_000;                
      217 +                                                              
      218 +/// Recognise `COUNT(*) GROUP BY date_bin(timestamp) [, <field
          +>]` and build the                                             
      219 +/// timeline node, or `None` to fall back.                    
      220 +fn try_build_timeline_node(plan: &LogicalPlan) -> Option<SfstT
          +imelineNode> {                                                
      221 +    let LogicalPlan::Aggregate(Aggregate {                    
      222 +        input,                                                
      223 +        group_expr,                                           
      224 +        aggr_expr,                                            
      225 +        schema,                                               
      226 +        ..                                                    
      227 +    }) = plan                                                 
      228 +    else {                                                    
      229 +        return None;                                          
      230 +    };                                                        
      231 +    let [agg] = aggr_expr.as_slice() else {                   
      232 +        return None;                                          
      233 +    };                                                        
      234 +    if !is_count_star(agg) || group_expr.is_empty() || group_e
          +xpr.len() > 2 {                                               
      235 +        return None;                                          
      236 +    }                                                         
      237 +                                                              
      238 +    // Identify the single date_bin group expr and an optional
          + scalar value                                                 
      239 +    // group expr, tracking their output-column positions (gro
          +up columns come                                               
      240 +    // first in the Aggregate schema, in group_expr order).   
      241 +    let mut date_bin: Option<(usize, i64, i64)> = None; // (po
          +s, stride_ns, origin_ns)                                      
      242 +    let mut value: Option<(usize, String)> = None; // (pos, fi
          +eld)                                                          
      243 +    for (i, g) in group_expr.iter().enumerate() {             
      244 +        if let Some((stride, origin)) = parse_date_bin(g) {   
      245 +            if date_bin.is_some() {                           
      246 +                return None;                                  
      247 +            }                                                 
      248 +            date_bin = Some((i, stride, origin));             
      249 +        } else if let Some(name) = column_name(g) {           
      250 +            if value.is_some() {                              
      251 +                return None;                                  
      252 +            }                                                 
      253 +            value = Some((i, name));                          
      254 +        } else {                                              
      255 +            return None;                                      
      256 +        }                                                     
      257 +    }                                                         
      258 +    let (time_pos, stride_ns, origin_ns) = date_bin?;         
      259 +                                                              
      260 +    let mut provider = None;                                  
      261 +    let (table, predicates) = scan_table(input.as_ref(), &mut 
          +provider)?;                                                   
      262 +    let sfst_schema = table.sfst_schema();                    
      263 +                                                              
      264 +    // Optional value field: low/mid scalar only (timeline err
          +ors on high-card,                                             
      265 +    // and a list column's grouping is not a per-value timelin
          +e).                                                           
      266 +    let value_field = match &value {                          
      267 +        Some((_, name)) => {                                  
      268 +            let spec = sfst_schema.specs.iter().find(|s| &s.na
          +me == name)?;                                                 
      269 +            if spec.kind == ColKind::List || spec.tier == Fiel
          +dTier::High {                                                 
      270 +                return None;                                  
      271 +            }                                                 
      272 +            Some((name.clone(), spec.kind))                   
      273 +        }                                                     
      274 +        None => None,                                         
      275 +    };                                                        
      276 +                                                              
      277 +    // Translate WHERE: equality predicates (not on the value 
          +field, which                                                  
      278 +    // timeline excludes from its own histogram) + a time wind
          +ow clamping the grid.                                         
      279 +    let mut eq_filter: Vec<(String, String)> = Vec::new();    
      280 +    let mut lo = i64::MIN;                                    
      281 +    let mut hi = i64::MAX;                                    
      282 +    for pred in &predicates {                                 
      283 +        match pushdown::classify(pred, sfst_schema) {         
      284 +            Some(Pushable::Equals { field, value: v }) => {   
      285 +                if value.as_ref().is_some_and(|(_, f)| f == &f
          +ield) {                                                       
      286 +                    return None;                              
      287 +                }                                             
      288 +                eq_filter.push((field, v));                   
      289 +            }                                                 
      290 +            Some(Pushable::TimeLo(v)) => lo = lo.max(v),      
      291 +            Some(Pushable::TimeHi(v)) => hi = hi.min(v),      
      292 +            None => return None,                              
      293 +        }                                                     
      294 +    }                                                         
      295 +                                                              
      296 +    // Size the grid to the data range (clamped by the WHERE w
          +indow) and align it                                           
      297 +    // to date_bin's boundaries: bucket k starts at origin + k
          +*stride.                                                      
      298 +    let (min_ts, max_ts) = table.ts_bounds();                 
      299 +    let range_start = min_ts.max(lo);                         
      300 +    let range_end = max_ts.saturating_add(1).min(hi);         
      301 +    let (bucket_start_ns, num_buckets) = if range_end <= range
          +_start {                                                      
      302 +        (origin_ns, 0)                                        
      303 +    } else {                                                  
      304 +        let start = origin_ns + (range_start - origin_ns).div_
          +euclid(stride_ns) * stride_ns;                                
      305 +        let n = (((range_end - start) as i128 + stride_ns as i
          +128 - 1) / stride_ns as i128) as usize;                       
      306 +        (start, n)                                            
      307 +    };                                                        
      308 +    if num_buckets > MAX_TIMELINE_BUCKETS {                   
      309 +        return None;                                          
      310 +    }                                                         
      311 +                                                              
      312 +    Some(SfstTimelineNode {                                   
      313 +        data: table.data(),                                   
      314 +        stride_ns,                                            
      315 +        bucket_start_ns,                                      
      316 +        num_buckets,                                          
      317 +        value_field,                                          
      318 +        eq_filter,                                            
      319 +        time_pos,                                             
      320 +        value_pos: value.as_ref().map(|(i, _)| *i),           
      321 +        group_cols: group_expr.len(),                         
      322 +        schema: schema.clone(),                               
      323 +    })                                                        
      324 +}                                                             
      325 +                                                              
      326 +/// Parse `date_bin(<interval>, <timestamp-col>[, <origin>])` 
          +over the timestamp                                            
      327 +/// column into `(stride_ns, origin_ns)`. Rejects month-based 
          +(variable-width)                                              
      328 +/// strides and any non-timestamp source.                     
      329 +fn parse_date_bin(expr: &Expr) -> Option<(i64, i64)> {        
      330 +    let inner = match expr {                                  
      331 +        Expr::Alias(a) => a.expr.as_ref(),                    
      332 +        e => e,                                               
      333 +    };                                                        
      334 +    let Expr::ScalarFunction(sf) = inner else {               
      335 +        return None;                                          
      336 +    };                                                        
      337 +    if sf.func.name() != "date_bin" {                         
      338 +        return None;                                          
      339 +    }                                                         
      340 +    if column_name(sf.args.get(1)?).as_deref() != Some(crate::
          +schema::TS_COLUMN) {                                          
      341 +        return None;                                          
      342 +    }                                                         
      343 +    let stride_ns = match sf.args.first()? {                  
      344 +        Expr::Literal(ScalarValue::IntervalMonthDayNano(Some(v
          +)), _) => {                                                   
      345 +            if v.months != 0 {                                
      346 +                return None; // calendar-month buckets are not
          + fixed-width                                                  
      347 +            }                                                 
      348 +            (v.days as i64)                                   
      349 +                .checked_mul(86_400_000_000_000)?             
      350 +                .checked_add(v.nanoseconds)?                  
      351 +        }                                                     
      352 +        _ => return None,                                     
      353 +    };                                                        
      354 +    if stride_ns <= 0 {                                       
      355 +        return None;                                          
      356 +    }                                                         
      357 +    let origin_ns = match sf.args.get(2) {                    
      358 +        None => 0,                                            
      359 +        Some(Expr::Literal(ScalarValue::TimestampNanosecond(So
          +me(o), _), _)) => *o,                                         
      360 +        Some(_) => return None,                               
      361 +    };                                                        
      362 +    Some((stride_ns, origin_ns))                              
      363 +}                                                             
      364 +                                                              
      365  // ── Logical node ───────────────────────────────────────────
           ───────────────

      694 +                                                              
      695 +impl fmt::Debug for SfstTimelineNode {                        
      696 +    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
      697 +        self.fmt_for_explain(f)                               
      698 +    }                                                         
      699 +}                                                             
      700 +                                                              
      701 +impl PartialEq for SfstTimelineNode {                         
      702 +    fn eq(&self, o: &Self) -> bool {                          
      703 +        Arc::ptr_eq(&self.data, &o.data)                      
      704 +            && self.stride_ns == o.stride_ns                  
      705 +            && self.bucket_start_ns == o.bucket_start_ns      
      706 +            && self.num_buckets == o.num_buckets              
      707 +            && self.value_name() == o.value_name()            
      708 +            && self.eq_filter == o.eq_filter                  
      709 +            && self.time_pos == o.time_pos                    
      710 +            && self.value_pos == o.value_pos                  
      711 +            && self.group_cols == o.group_cols                
      712 +    }                                                         
      713 +}                                                             
      714 +impl Eq for SfstTimelineNode {}                               
      715 +                                                              
      716 +impl Hash for SfstTimelineNode {                              
      717 +    fn hash<H: Hasher>(&self, state: &mut H) {                
      718 +        (Arc::as_ptr(&self.data) as *const () as usize).hash(s
          +tate);                                                        
      719 +        self.stride_ns.hash(state);                           
      720 +        self.bucket_start_ns.hash(state);                     
      721 +        self.num_buckets.hash(state);                         
      722 +        self.value_name().hash(state);                        
      723 +        self.eq_filter.hash(state);                           
      724 +        self.time_pos.hash(state);                            
      725 +        self.value_pos.hash(state);                           
      726 +        self.group_cols.hash(state);                          
      727 +    }                                                         
      728 +}                                                             
      729 +                                                              
      730 +impl PartialOrd for SfstTimelineNode {                        
      731 +    fn partial_cmp(&self, o: &Self) -> Option<std::cmp::Orderi
          +ng> {                                                         
      732 +        (                                                     
      733 +            Arc::as_ptr(&self.data) as *const () as usize,    
      734 +            self.stride_ns,                                   
      735 +            self.bucket_start_ns,                             
      736 +            self.num_buckets,                                 
      737 +            self.value_name(),                                
      738 +            &self.eq_filter,                                  
      739 +            self.time_pos,                                    
      740 +            self.value_pos,                                   
      741 +            self.group_cols,                                  
      742 +        )                                                     
      743 +            .partial_cmp(&(                                   
      744 +                Arc::as_ptr(&o.data) as *const () as usize,   
      745 +                o.stride_ns,                                  
      746 +                o.bucket_start_ns,                            
      747 +                o.num_buckets,                                
      748 +                o.value_name(),                               
      749 +                &o.eq_filter,                                 
      750 +                o.time_pos,                                   
      751 +                o.value_pos,                                  
      752 +                o.group_cols,                                 
      753 +            ))                                                
      754 +    }                                                         
      755 +}                                                             
      756 +                                                              
      757 +impl UserDefinedLogicalNodeCore for SfstTimelineNode {        
      758 +    fn name(&self) -> &str {                                  
      759 +        "SfstTimeline"                                        
      760 +    }                                                         
      761 +    fn inputs(&self) -> Vec<&LogicalPlan> {                   
      762 +        vec![]                                                
      763 +    }                                                         
      764 +    fn schema(&self) -> &DFSchemaRef {                        
      765 +        &self.schema                                          
      766 +    }                                                         
      767 +    fn expressions(&self) -> Vec<Expr> {                      
      768 +        vec![]                                                
      769 +    }                                                         
      770 +    fn fmt_for_explain(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter) -> fmt::
          +Result {                                                      
      771 +        match self.value_name() {                             
      772 +            Some(field) => write!(                            
      773 +                f,                                            
      774 +                "SfstTimeline: date_bin({}ns) x \"{}\", count(
          +*) via timeline bitmaps",                                     
      775 +                self.stride_ns, field                         
      776 +            ),                                                
      777 +            None => write!(                                   
      778 +                f,                                            
      779 +                "SfstTimeline: date_bin({}ns), count(*) via ti
          +meline bitmaps",                                              
      780 +                self.stride_ns                                
      781 +            ),                                                
      782 +        }                                                     
      783 +    }                                                         
      784 +    fn with_exprs_and_inputs(&self, _exprs: Vec<Expr>, _inputs
          +: Vec<LogicalPlan>) -> Result<Self> {                         
      785 +        Ok(self.clone())                                      
      786 +    }                                                         
      787 +}                                                             
      788 +                                                              
      789 +struct SfstTimelineExec {                                     
      790 +    data: Arc<Vec<u8>>,                                       
      791 +    stride_ns: i64,                                           
      792 +    bucket_start_ns: i64,                                     
      793 +    num_buckets: usize,                                       
      794 +    value_field: Option<(String, ColKind)>,                   
      795 +    eq_filter: Vec<(String, String)>,                         
      796 +    time_pos: usize,                                          
      797 +    value_pos: Option<usize>,                                 
      798 +    group_cols: usize,                                        
      799 +    schema: SchemaRef,                                        
      800 +    cache: Arc<PlanProperties>,                               
      801 +}                                                             
      802 +                                                              
      803 +impl SfstTimelineExec {                                       
      804 +    #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]                      
      805 +    fn new(                                                   
      806 +        data: Arc<Vec<u8>>,                                   
      807 +        stride_ns: i64,                                       
      808 +        bucket_start_ns: i64,                                 
      809 +        num_buckets: usize,                                   
      810 +        value_field: Option<(String, ColKind)>,               
      811 +        eq_filter: Vec<(String, String)>,                     
      812 +        time_pos: usize,                                      
      813 +        value_pos: Option<usize>,                             
      814 +        group_cols: usize,                                    
      815 +        schema: SchemaRef,                                    
      816 +    ) -> Self {                                               
      817 +        let cache = Arc::new(PlanProperties::new(             
      818 +            EquivalenceProperties::new(schema.clone()),       
      819 +            Partitioning::UnknownPartitioning(1),             
      820 +            EmissionType::Incremental,                        
      821 +            Boundedness::Bounded,                             
      822 +        ));                                                   
      823 +        Self {                                                
      824 +            data,                                             
      825 +            stride_ns,                                        
      826 +            bucket_start_ns,                                  
      827 +            num_buckets,                                      
      828 +            value_field,                                      
      829 +            eq_filter,                                        
      830 +            time_pos,                                         
      831 +            value_pos,                                        
      832 +            group_cols,                                       
      833 +            schema,                                           
      834 +            cache,                                            
      835 +        }                                                     
      836 +    }                                                         
      837 +                                                              
      838 +    fn build_batch(&self) -> Result<RecordBatch> {            
      839 +        let reader = IndexReader::open(&self.data).map_err(exe
          +c_err)?;                                                      
      840 +        let mut filter = Filter::new();                       
      841 +        for (k, v) in &self.eq_filter {                       
      842 +            filter = filter.select(k.clone(), v.clone());     
      843 +        }                                                     
      844 +        let compiled = reader.compile_filter(&filter, None).ma
          +p_err(exec_err)?;                                             
      845 +        let grid = Grid::new(self.bucket_start_ns, self.stride
          +_ns, self.num_buckets);                                       
      846 +                                                              
      847 +        let mut times: Vec<i64> = Vec::new();                 
      848 +        let mut values: Vec<Option<String>> = Vec::new();     
      849 +        let mut counts: Vec<i64> = Vec::new();                
      850 +                                                              
      851 +        match &self.value_field {                             
      852 +            Some((field, _)) => {                             
      853 +                // 2-D: emit one row per (bucket, value) with 
          +a positive count,                                             
      854 +                // plus the absent-field (NULL) group from `un
          +set`.                                                         
      855 +                let tl = reader                               
      856 +                    .timeline(field, &compiled, grid)         
      857 +                    .map_err(exec_err)?;                      
      858 +                for (b, bucket) in tl.buckets.iter().enumerate
          +() {                                                          
      859 +                    let ts = self.bucket_start_ns + b as i64 *
          + self.stride_ns;                                              
      860 +                    for (j, dim) in tl.dimensions.iter().enume
          +rate() {                                                      
      861 +                        if bucket.counts[j] > 0 {             
      862 +                            times.push(ts);                   
      863 +                            values.push(Some(dim.clone()));   
      864 +                            counts.push(bucket.counts[j] as i6
          +4);                                                           
      865 +                        }                                     
      866 +                    }                                         
      867 +                    if bucket.unset > 0 {                     
      868 +                        times.push(ts);                       
      869 +                        values.push(None);                    
      870 +                        counts.push(bucket.unset as i64);     
      871 +                    }                                         
      872 +                }                                             
      873 +            }                                                 
      874 +            None => {                                         
      875 +                // 1-D: one row per non-empty bucket.         
      876 +                let totals = reader.timeline_totals(&compiled,
          + grid).map_err(exec_err)?;                                    
      877 +                for (b, total) in totals.iter().enumerate() { 
      878 +                    if *total > 0 {                           
      879 +                        times.push(self.bucket_start_ns + b as
          + i64 * self.stride_ns);                                       
      880 +                        counts.push(*total as i64);           
      881 +                    }                                         
      882 +                }                                             
      883 +            }                                                 
      884 +        }                                                     
      885 +                                                              
      886 +        // Assemble columns in the Aggregate's output order.  
      887 +        let mut cols: Vec<Option<ArrayRef>> = (0..=self.group_
          +cols).map(|_| None).collect();                                
      888 +        cols[self.time_pos] = Some(Arc::new(TimestampNanosecon
          +dArray::from(times)) as ArrayRef);                            
      889 +        cols[self.group_cols] = Some(Arc::new(Int64Array::from
          +(counts)) as ArrayRef);                                       
      890 +        if let (Some(vpos), Some((_, kind))) = (self.value_pos
          +, &self.value_field) {                                        
      891 +            cols[vpos] = Some(build_opt_column(*kind, &values)
          +);                                                            
      892 +        }                                                     
      893 +        let columns: Vec<ArrayRef> = cols.into_iter().map(Opti
          +on::unwrap).collect();                                        
      894 +        RecordBatch::try_new(self.schema.clone(), columns).map
          +_err(Into::into)                                              
      895 +    }                                                         
      896 +}                                                             
      897 +                                                              
      898 +/// Build a typed value column from optional string values (NU
          +LL = absent-field                                             
      899 +/// group). Mirrors the group-key typing used elsewhere.      
      900 +fn build_opt_column(kind: ColKind, values: &[Option<String>]) 
          +-> ArrayRef {                                                 
      901 +    match kind {                                              
      902 +        ColKind::Int => {                                     
      903 +            let mut b = Int64Builder::new();                  
      904 +            for v in values {                                 
      905 +                b.append_option(v.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.pars
          +e::<i64>().ok()));                                            
      906 +            }                                                 
      907 +            Arc::new(b.finish())                              
      908 +        }                                                     
      909 +        ColKind::Double => {                                  
      910 +            let mut b = Float64Builder::new();                
      911 +            for v in values {                                 
      912 +                b.append_option(v.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.pars
          +e::<f64>().ok()));                                            
      913 +            }                                                 
      914 +            Arc::new(b.finish())                              
      915 +        }                                                     
      916 +        ColKind::Bool => {                                    
      917 +            let mut b = BooleanBuilder::new();                
      918 +            for v in values {                                 
      919 +                b.append_option(v.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.pars
          +e::<bool>().ok()));                                           
      920 +            }                                                 
      921 +            Arc::new(b.finish())                              
      922 +        }                                                     
      923 +        // Str, and List (excluded from pushdown) → Utf8.     
      924 +        _ => {                                                
      925 +            let mut b = StringBuilder::new();                 
      926 +            for v in values {                                 
      927 +                match v {                                     
      928 +                    Some(s) => b.append_value(s),             
      929 +                    None => b.append_null(),                  
      930 +                }                                             
      931 +            }                                                 
      932 +            Arc::new(b.finish())                              
      933 +        }                                                     
      934 +    }                                                         
      935 +}                                                             
      936 +                                                              
      937 +impl fmt::Debug for SfstTimelineExec {                        
      938 +    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
      939 +        write!(f, "SfstTimelineExec")                         
      940 +    }                                                         
      941 +}                                                             
      942 +                                                              
      943 +impl DisplayAs for SfstTimelineExec {                         
      944 +    fn fmt_as(&self, _t: DisplayFormatType, f: &mut fmt::Forma
          +tter) -> fmt::Result {                                        
      945 +        write!(f, "SfstTimelineExec: {} bucket(s) (timeline bi
          +tmaps)", self.num_buckets)                                    
      946 +    }                                                         
      947 +}                                                             
      948 +                                                              
      949 +impl ExecutionPlan for SfstTimelineExec {                     
      950 +    fn name(&self) -> &str {                                  
      951 +        "SfstTimelineExec"                                    
      952 +    }                                                         
      953 +    fn properties(&self) -> &Arc<PlanProperties> {            
      954 +        &self.cache                                           
      955 +    }                                                         
      956 +    fn children(&self) -> Vec<&Arc<dyn ExecutionPlan>> {      
      957 +        vec![]                                                
      958 +    }                                                         
      959 +    fn with_new_children(                                     
      960 +        self: Arc<Self>,                                      
      961 +        _children: Vec<Arc<dyn ExecutionPlan>>,               
      962 +    ) -> Result<Arc<dyn ExecutionPlan>> {                     
      963 +        Ok(self)                                              
      964 +    }                                                         
      965 +    fn execute(                                               
      966 +        &self,                                                
      967 +        _partition: usize,                                    
      968 +        _context: Arc<TaskContext>,                           
      969 +    ) -> Result<SendableRecordBatchStream> {                  
      970 +        let batch = self.build_batch()?;                      
      971 +        Ok(Box::pin(MemoryStream::try_new(                    
      972 +            vec![batch],                                      
      973 +            self.schema.clone(),                              
      974 +            None,                                             
      975 +        )?))                                                  
      976 +    }                                                         
      977 +}                                                             
      978 +                                                              
      979  // ── Query planner (injects the extension planner) ──────────
           ───────────────────

      383 +// ── Stage D: timeline (date_bin) pushdown ──────────────────

      385 +/// `(bucket_ns, count)` rows from a 1-D `GROUP BY date_bin(..
          +.)`, sorted.                                                  
      386 +async fn timeline_1d(ctx: &SessionContext, sql: &str) -> Vec<(
          +i64, i64)> {                                                  
      387 +    use datafusion::arrow::array::AsArray;                    
      388 +    use datafusion::arrow::datatypes::{Int64Type, TimestampNan
          +osecondType};                                                 
      389 +                                                              
      390 +    let batches = ctx.sql(sql).await.unwrap().collect().await.
          +unwrap();                                                     
      391 +    let mut out = Vec::new();                                 
      392 +    for b in &batches {                                       
      393 +        let t = b.column(0).as_primitive::<TimestampNanosecond
          +Type>();                                                      
      394 +        let n = b.column(1).as_primitive::<Int64Type>();      
      395 +        for i in 0..b.num_rows() {                            
      396 +            out.push((t.value(i), n.value(i)));               
      397 +        }                                                     
      398 +    }                                                         
      399 +    out.sort();                                               
      400 +    out                                                       
      401 +}                                                             
      402 +                                                              
      403 +/// `(bucket_ns, value, count)` rows from a 2-D `GROUP BY date
          +_bin(...), field`.                                            
      404 +async fn timeline_2d(ctx: &SessionContext, sql: &str) -> Vec<(
          +i64, Option<String>, i64)> {                                  
      405 +    use datafusion::arrow::array::{Array, AsArray, StringArray
          +};                                                            
      406 +    use datafusion::arrow::datatypes::{Int64Type, TimestampNan
          +osecondType};                                                 
      407 +                                                              
      408 +    let batches = ctx.sql(sql).await.unwrap().collect().await.
          +unwrap();                                                     
      409 +    let mut out = Vec::new();                                 
      410 +    for b in &batches {                                       
      411 +        let t = b.column(0).as_primitive::<TimestampNanosecond
          +Type>();                                                      
      412 +        let g = b.column(1).as_any().downcast_ref::<StringArra
          +y>().unwrap();                                                
      413 +        let n = b.column(2).as_primitive::<Int64Type>();      
      414 +        for i in 0..b.num_rows() {                            
      415 +            let key = if g.is_null(i) {                       
      416 +                None                                          
      417 +            } else {                                          
      418 +                Some(g.value(i).to_string())                  
      419 +            };                                                
      420 +            out.push((t.value(i), key, n.value(i)));          
      421 +        }                                                     
      422 +    }                                                         
      423 +    out.sort();                                               
      424 +    out                                                       
      425 +}

      427 +/// 1-D time histogram: pushdown must fire and match the norma
          +l plan exactly                                                
      428 +/// (this is the date_bin bucket-alignment correctness check).
      429 +#[tokio::test]                                                
      430 +async fn timeline_1d_pushdown_matches_normal() {              
      431 +    let Some(path) = fixture() else { return };               
      432 +    let sql =                                                 
      433 +        r#"SELECT date_bin(INTERVAL '1 minute', timestamp) AS 
          +t, count(*) AS n FROM logs GROUP BY t"#;                      
      434 +

      436 +    let normal = ctx_with_fixture(&path).await;               
      437 +

      439 +    assert!(                                                  
      440 +        explain.contains("SfstTimeline"),                     
      441 +        "timeline pushdown should fire:\n{explain}"           
      442 +    );                                                        
      443 +                                                              
      444 +    let got = timeline_1d(&pushed, sql).await;                
      445 +    let expected = timeline_1d(&normal, sql).await;           
      446 +    assert_eq!(got, expected, "pushed timeline must equal the 
          +normal plan");                                                
      447 +    assert!(got.len() >= 2, "expected several time buckets"); 
      448 +}

      461 +    );

      463 +    let pushed = pushed_ctx(&path).await;

● Committed as 6962bc73cc. Timeline pushdown done.

  What landed

  - 1-D (SELECT date_bin('1 min', timestamp), COUNT(*) GROUP BY 1) → new sfst
  primitive IndexReader::timeline_totals (per-bucket matched counts).
  - 2-D (… date_bin(...), <field>, COUNT(*) GROUP BY 1, 2) → existing
  IndexReader::timeline, with its per-bucket unset becoming the NULL group.
  - The rule now parses date_bin's IntervalMonthDayNano stride (rejecting
  variable-width month intervals), aligns a Grid to date_bin's origin + 
  k*stride, sizes it to the file's time range (clamped by any WHERE time window,
  capped at 1M buckets → else fall back), and emits a timeline extension node.
  Same correctness gating/fallback as facets.

  Correctness — the bucket-alignment risk, verified

  Both tests pass: pushed result == the normal aggregation plan, for 1-D and 2-D
  (the latter including the per-bucket NULL/absent-field group). That's the
  proof the Grid boundaries match DataFusion's date_bin exactly.

  Performance

  1-D date_bin('10s'): 38.5ms → 18.1ms (~2×), identical results. Same big-O
  story — O(buckets) vs O(rows), widening with scale.

  State

  - Only new sfst API: timeline_totals (timeline already existed). No on-disk 
  format change.
  - clippy clean; 84 sfst lib + 12 integration + doctest pass.
  - Committed 6962bc73cc; SOW + design note updated. Excluded the two SOWs +
  run-ingest-v8.sh as before.

  What's left

  - Multi-field (non-time) GROUP BY — e.g. GROUP BY a, b on two attributes. Poor
  fit for the facet index (cross-product of value bitmaps can explode), so it
  stays a correct fallback. I'd leave it unless a concrete need appears.

  The DataFusion↔SFST integration now covers the full arc: scan, projection,
  filter/time pushdown (column-direct reads), and aggregation pushdown (facets +
  timeline) — all correctness-verified against oracles, no format change. Good
  stopping point unless you want the multi-field case or something new.

✻ Churned for 11m 46s

❯ As part of the entire sfst + datafusion investigation, did you identify any   
  SFST file format changes/additions that would be meaningful to add? Of        
  course, I'm talking about either something that will not blow the file size,  
  or something restructuring of the way we are storing existing data in the     
  file format.                                                                  

● Good question to ask retrospectively. Short version: the integration ran 
  almost entirely against the format as-is — KvId ranges, tiers, facets,
  timeline, time-ordering, and per-row scalar columns were all already there or
  derivable. I found no cheap "free win" addition worth making. But the work did
  surface two size-respecting restructures that would unlock optimizations the
  current encoding actively blocks, plus one real friction that has no cheap
  fix. I'll separate facts from speculation and flag size honestly (these
  hypotheticals aren't measured).

  What needed nothing (confirmed)

  - Per-chunk KvId ranges — you raised this; already derivable from META
  cardinalities (high_kv_id, no chunk decode). Not worth adding.
  - Tiers, facet/timeline counts, timestamp ordering, row count + ts min/max 
  stats — all already present.
  - Column-direct reads for low/mid fields are clean (per-value posting bitmaps
  → scatter, no row scan).

  Restructure 1 — typed columnar storage for high-frequency numeric/enum fields

  (reuses the existing v8 per-row-column mechanism — ColumnsTable + cold-region 
  chunks like OBTS/FLAG)
  - What it unlocks: DataFusion reads such a field as a native typed Arrow 
  column with zero row-major SB decode, and it enables typed/range predicate
  pushdown (Stage C) directly on the column.
  - Size (reasoning, not measured): for a numeric/fixed-width field, an inline
  per-row value (e.g. 4 bytes) is ≈ the 4-byte KvId code it has in SB today, and
  you drop its FST/arena dict entry — so plausibly size-neutral or smaller for
  that field.
  - Honest caveat: this is only true for numeric/fixed-width, high-frequency
  fields. For string or sparse fields, the current dict+codes is smaller (inline
  loses dictionary dedup / adds NULL overhead) — don't promote those. So it's a
  scoped, per-field-type promotion, not a blanket change. It's the low-risk
  slice of the Stage B columnar idea you froze, justified by exactly what the
  integration wanted.

  Restructure 2 — order-preserving encoding for typed values

  - The blocker (fact): values are stored as key=value strings, and the FST/HF
  arenas are sorted lexically. For numbers, lexical ≠ numeric order (n=9 sorts
  after n=50). So WHERE n > 500 / BETWEEN can't binary-search the index — that's
  why Stage C numeric pushdown isn't free today.
  - The change: encode numeric/typed field values in an order-preserving form
  (e.g. fixed-width/zero-padded or a sortable numeric encoding) so the
  already-sorted FST/arena supports numeric range scans.
  - Size: neutral (it's a re-encoding, same data). Enables Stage C range
  pushdown cleanly. Caveat: only helps typed/numeric fields; pure-string fields
  unaffected.

  The one real friction with no cheap fix

  - High-card column reads still scan the stream batches (SB, 26%) — high-card
  fields have no per-value position bitmaps (only batch masks), so column-direct
  reads must scan SB row-major to map rows→value.
  - Why there's no cheap fix: adding per-value bitmaps back for high-card would
  blow up the file (near-unique values → one near-empty bitmap each — exactly
  why v2 removed them). The only real remedy is inline columnar storage for
  high-card values, which is the full Stage B redesign (bigger, riskier,
  possible size shift) — not size-respecting in the cheap sense.

  What I would not touch

  - The 68% high-card index is near-unique high-entropy strings (cert hashes,
  DIDs, URIs) — irreducible; no reorganization shrinks it.
  - Re-adding high-card bitmaps — size disaster (see above).

  Bottom line / recommendation

  - No cheap additive win exists — the format already served the integration
  well.
  - The two meaningful, size-respecting moves are restructures, and they map
  exactly to the deferred stages: #1 → scoped Stage B (typed columnar for
  numeric/enum fields), #2 → enables Stage C (range pushdown). Both are real,
  both your call.
  - If you ever revisit the format, I'd rank #2 first (clearly size-neutral,
  unblocks a whole predicate class, smaller change) then #1 (needs a per-field
  promotion policy + careful size measurement on real data before committing).

  These are design options, not decisions — and #1's size claim in particular
  I'd want to measure on out-v9.sfst before trusting it.

✻ Cogitated for 2m 12s

❯ Can you walk me through how datafusion knows the "table" of a SFST file and   
  it's "columns"?                                                               

● Here's the end-to-end flow, from on-disk bytes to DataFusion knowing FROM logs
  and its columns.

  1. The "table" — assigned at registration, not in the file

  One SFST file = one table. The name isn't stored in the file; you choose it
  when you register:

  let table = SfstTable::open_path("out.sfst")?;   // the file becomes a 
  provider
  ctx.register_table("logs", Arc::new(table))?;     // "logs" → this 
  TableProvider

  register_table puts "logs" → Arc<dyn TableProvider> into DataFusion's catalog.
  From then on, FROM logs resolves to our SfstTable. The file itself has no
  notion of "logs" — it's just the table body; the name is a catalog label.

  2. Opening the file → reading the on-disk descriptor

  SfstTable::open (table.rs) opens an sfst::IndexReader, which parses the
  container TOC and reads the META chunk. Per FORMAT.md, META holds:

  Metadata { tree: SchemaTree, histogram, id_ranges, columns: Vec<ColumnEntry> }

  The SchemaTree (v9) is the typed, on-disk field descriptor: an arena of nodes
  where each leaf is a (path, ValueKind) carrying the field's cardinality +
  tier. A path collapses array indices to [] (e.g. attributes.tags[]). This tree
  is the source of the columns.

  3. Deriving the columns — SfstSchema::build (schema.rs)

  This turns the tree into an Arrow SchemaRef:

  1. Types map: reader.tree().derive_scalar_kinds() → Vec<(path, ValueKind)>
  (the coalesced scalar kind per path).
  2. Leading timestamp column: Field::new("timestamp", Timestamp(Nanosecond), 
  nullable=false) — synthetic, sourced from the TIMS chunk (not an attribute;
  it's the frozen per-record time, also the table's sort order).
  3. One column per field: loop reader.field_table().iter() (the flat view
  derived from the tree). For each FieldEntry:
    - name = the dotted path verbatim (e.g. attributes.bluesky.commit.cid).
    - type = ValueKind → Arrow: Int→Int64, Double→Float64, Str/Bytes→Utf8,
  Bool→Boolean; a [] path → List<Utf8>.
    - nullable = true (logs are sparse — most rows lack most fields).
    - we also stash a ColumnSpec{name, kind, tier} parallel to each field (used
  later for the value pivot + facet eligibility).

  Result: Schema = [ timestamp, <col per leaf path…> ], wrapped in an
  Arc<Schema> and cached on the SfstTable.

  4. How DataFusion consumes it

  The TableProvider trait exposes it:

  fn schema(&self) -> SchemaRef { self.schema.schema.clone() }

  When you run SELECT "attributes.bluesky.commit.cid", timestamp FROM logs WHERE
  …:
  - the SQL planner resolves logs → the provider → calls schema(),
  - uses it to validate column names, assign types, and bind
  "attributes.bluesky.commit.cid" to column index N,
  - then calls scan(projection=[N, 0], filters, limit) with the resolved column
  indices into that schema.

  So every column name/type DataFusion knows comes straight from the SchemaTree
  in the file's META chunk, plus the one synthetic timestamp column.

  Concrete example

  A file whose SchemaTree has leaves body.commit.record.text (Str, high-card),
  attributes.tags[] (Str under an array), severity_number (Int) yields this
  table:

  ┌─────────────────────────┬───────────────┬────────────────────────┐
  │         column          │  Arrow type   │         source         │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ timestamp               │ Timestamp(ns) │ TIMS chunk (synthetic) │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ body.commit.record.text │ Utf8          │ tree leaf (Str)        │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ attributes.tags[]       │ List<Utf8>    │ tree leaf, [] → list   │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ severity_number         │ Int64         │ tree leaf (Int)        │
  └─────────────────────────┴───────────────┴────────────────────────┘

  A row that has no severity_number simply reads NULL there — the column exists
  in the schema (the path was seen somewhere in the stream), it's just absent
  for that row.

  One nuance worth calling out: the schema is the union of every field path ever
  seen in that stream (that's what the tree records), so a wide sparse log file
  produces a wide table where any given row populates only a subset of columns.
  That's also why every attribute column is nullable and why projection
  pushdown matters — you only ever build the columns a query actually asks for.

● Works. Here's how to use it.

  Command

  cd /home/vk/repos/nd/sjr-tree8/src/crates
  cargo run -p sfst-datafusion --example sql -- <path/to/file.sfst> "<SQL>"
  - The file is registered as table logs.
  - Quote dotted column names: "attributes.bluesky.commit.cid" (otherwise SQL
  reads the dots as table.column).
  - The timestamp column is the per-record time (also the table's sort order).
  - It uses the pushdown-enabled context, so COUNT(*) GROUP BY <field> and
  date_bin GROUP BY hit the facet/timeline bitmaps automatically.

  Example queries (against your fixture)

  F=/home/vk/repos/tmp/ng/out-v9.sfst

  # scan + filter + projection + limit
  cargo run -p sfst-datafusion --example sql -- $F \
    'SELECT timestamp, "body.commit.record.text" FROM logs
     WHERE "attributes.bluesky.did" = '"'"'did:plc:xxxx'"'"' ORDER BY timestamp 
  LIMIT 20'

  # GROUP BY a field  → facet bitmaps
  cargo run -p sfst-datafusion --example sql -- $F \
    'SELECT "attributes.bluesky.account.status" AS status, count(*) AS n
     FROM logs GROUP BY status ORDER BY n DESC'

  # log volume over time → timeline bitmaps
  cargo run -p sfst-datafusion --example sql -- $F \
    "SELECT date_bin(INTERVAL '1 minute', timestamp) AS minute, count(*) AS n
     FROM logs GROUP BY minute ORDER BY minute"

  # inspect the schema / available columns
  cargo run -p sfst-datafusion --example sql -- $F 'SELECT * FROM logs LIMIT 1'

  Tips

  - To see what got pushed down, prefix with EXPLAIN: EXPLAIN SELECT … GROUP BY 
  … shows SfstFacetExec / SfstTimelineExec (pushed) vs AggregateExec (normal).
  - Don't use --release — the workspace release profile is lto=fat and will take
  a very long time to build; the default dev build is what I've been running.
  - To discover column names, DESCRIBE isn't wired, but SELECT * FROM logs LIMIT
  1 shows them, or run the lower-level cargo run -p sfst --example inspect -- 
  summary $F for the field list with cardinalities/tiers.

  One rendering note from the demo: the blank status row (500,751) is the
  absent-field / NULL group — rows that don't carry that attribute — which the
  facet pushdown reconstructs to match the normal plan.

※ recap: Goal: run SQL over SFST files via DataFusion. We built the `sfst-datafusion` crate with scan, filter, projection, and facet/timeline aggregation pushdown — all committed and tested. Just enabled `information_schema`. Next: your call on what to build or query next.

❯ Any field/colum we can ran range query on?                                    

● Good question — and it splits cleanly into "works and is index-accelerated" vs
  "works but scans." Let me find the numeric columns (now that
  information_schema is on) and demonstrate both.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Lots of numeric columns. Here's the key distinction — let me demonstrate it
  concretely on body.account.seq (Int64) and timestamp:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Clear answer: you can run a range query on any column — they all return 
  correct results — but only timestamp is index-accelerated.

  The two cases (just demonstrated)

  - timestamp — range works and is pushed into the index. WHERE timestamp >= 
  TIMESTAMP '…' → EXPLAIN shows the SfstExec directly under the aggregate with
  no FilterExec — the time window is applied inside the scan via the timestamp
  index. Fast, O(window), not O(rows).
  - Numeric columns (Int64/Float64, e.g. body.account.seq, the 
  *.activity.*/*.aggregates.* counters) — range works but scans. WHERE 
  "body.account.seq" > 0 returned 98 correctly, but EXPLAIN shows FilterExec: 
  body.account.seq@0 > 0 sitting above SfstExec — DataFusion reads the column
  (fast, column-direct) then filters every row itself. The index isn't consulted
  for the comparison.
  - String columns: same as numeric — col > 'abc' works via scan + filter
  (lexical), not pushed.

  Why only timestamp is pushed

  - Time is the one dimension SFST is physically sorted on (the TIMS index), so
  a range maps to a position window — that's the D5 pushdown I built.
  - Numeric/value range pushdown is Stage C (not built). And there's a format
  reason it isn't free: attribute values are stored as lexically-sorted strings,
  so the index can't binary-search a numeric range (9 sorts after 50). That's
  exactly the "order-preserving value encoding" restructure I flagged earlier as
  the prerequisite.

  Practical takeaway

  - WHERE timestamp BETWEEN … AND … → use freely; it's accelerated.
  - WHERE <numeric col> > x / BETWEEN → works and is correct; on this 500k file
  it's a fast column scan + filter (sub-second), just not index-pushed.
  Combining it with a timestamp range is the best of both — the time window
  prunes via the index first, then the numeric filter runs on the survivors.

  If you want numeric range queries actually pushed into the index, that's Stage
  C + the value-encoding restructure — say the word and I'll scope it.

  SELECT count(*) FROM logs WHERE "body.data.seen" BETWEEN 1782673900 AND
  1782674000

  filter.

  Query 2 — the hybrid (timestamp pushed + numeric on survivors)

  SELECT count(*) FROM logs
  WHERE timestamp >= TIMESTAMP '2026-06-28T19:14:00' AND "body.data.seen" >
  1782674100
  → 73,083 rows. The EXPLAIN proves the split exactly as described:
  FilterExec: body.data.seen@0 > 1782674100      ← numeric filter, on survivors
      SfstExec: projection=1 cols                ← timestamp window pushed
  INSIDE here

  pushed into SfstExec, so the time index narrows the rows first, and DataFusion
  only re-checks body.data.seen on what survives. That's the
  index-prune-then-scan-filter behavior in action.

  Takeaway: numeric range queries work on any numeric column today; pairing them
  with a timestamp range is the efficient pattern — the time bound is the part
  that hits the index. If you want the numeric bound itself pushed into the
  index, that's the Stage C + value-encoding work I flagged.

✻ Crunched for 1m 38s

❯ > that's the Stage C + value-encoding work I flagged.                         
                                                                                
  What's this about? Can you provide more info?                                 

● Good to dig in — and I need to correct an overstatement I made earlier: I
  called the value-encoding a "prerequisite," but it's really an optimization.
  Let me lay out both pieces accurately.

  The two pieces

  Stage C = the DataFusion-side pushdown (the missing rule). Today our
  pushdown.rs::classify recognizes exactly two predicate shapes as
  index-pushable: col = 'string' and timestamp <cmp> <ts>. A numeric comparison
  like n > 500 falls through to Unsupported → DataFusion keeps a FilterExec and
  scans (what you saw with body.data.seen). Stage C adds a rule arm: recognize
  >, <, >=, <=, BETWEEN on a numeric column and translate it into an SFST index
  lookup.

  The value-encoding question = how the index can answer a range, which
  determines whether Stage C is fast or just enumerating.

  Why a range is awkward against the current index

  SFST stores values as key=value strings, and a field's values live in an
  FST/arena sorted lexically. Lexically, "9" sorts after "500" ('9' > '5'). So
  the sorted order ≠ numeric order, and you can't binary-search "all values >
  500".

  Two ways to do Stage C — with honest trade-offs

  (A) Enumerate-and-test — works on the current format, no change.
  SFST already has machinery that walks a field's distinct values and tests each
  (that's how regex/Pattern matching works in field_values_or). A numeric range
  is the same shape: enumerate the field's values, keep those where parse(v) is
  in range, union their posting bitmaps → matched rows.
  - For low/mid-card fields this is genuinely index-accelerated — you touch the
  field's bounded value set + bitmaps, not the rows. Cost ≈ O(field
  cardinality), which is small by definition for low/mid tiers.
  - For high-card fields it's poor — enumerating ~all values, and high-card has
  no per-value bitmaps (needs the SB scan).
  - This is the low-risk path: it makes WHERE severity_number > 500
  index-accelerated for low/mid numeric fields with zero format change.

  (B) Order-preserving value index — faster, but a real trade-off.
  To turn the range into a binary-search (O(log + matches)) you need the field's
  values stored so lexical order == numeric order (e.g. big-endian sign-flipped
  integers). Then > 500 is a contiguous suffix.
  - The catch I glossed over: those key=value strings are also what
  materialize_rows/display return. If you re-encode values into sortable bytes,
  every read path must decode them back for display — that's invasive, not free.
  - The non-invasive variant is a sidecar typed index (sorted numeric values +
  bitmap refs) leaving the display strings intact — but that's an addition with
  a size cost, not the size-neutral restructure I implied.
  - So (B) is a meaningful perf/scale win but carries either a display-decode
  cost or a size cost. Not the clean freebie I made it sound like.

  Corrected bottom line

  - I was wrong to call value-encoding a "prerequisite." Stage C for low/mid 
  numeric fields is achievable on the current format via enumerate-and-test
  (path A), reusing existing value-enumeration code — index-accelerated, no
  format change.
  - The value-encoding/sidecar (path B) is an optional optimization that makes
  ranges sub-linear and extends them to high-card fields, but with a genuine
  trade-off (display decode or extra size) — so it's a deliberate decision, not
  an obvious win.

  If you wanted it

  - Scope-minimal: Stage C path A — add a Range arm to classify, mark it Exact
  only for low/mid Int/Double scalar fields, and a small IndexReader method that
  enumerates the field's values in range and unions bitmaps (mirrors the
  existing pattern path). No format change. This would push body.data.seen-style
  ranges into the index for low/mid fields.
  - Honest caveat: body.data.seen in your fixture is actually high-card (358k
  near-distinct unix-second values), so path A wouldn't help that specific
  column much — it's the low/mid numeric fields (counters, enums, small-range
  ints) that benefit. High-card numeric ranges still want path B or stay a scan.

  Want me to build Stage C path A (low/mid numeric range pushdown, no format
  change), or leave it?

✻ Churned for 1m 21s

❯ Can we calc the man/min/avg value of  `body.data.seen` for a given time       
  range?                                                                        
● Yes — that's a standard aggregate with the time range index-pushed and
  body.data.seen scanned on the survivors. Running it for a 2-minute window:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Yes — works cleanly. For timestamp in [19:12:00, 19:14:00):

  ┌─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────┐
  │             metric              │     value      │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ count (non-null body.data.seen) │ 83,356         │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ min                             │ 1782673920.019 │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ max                             │ 1782674039.903 │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ avg                             │ 1782673971.627 │
  └─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────┘

  (Those are unix-second values; the ~120s min→max span lines up with the
  2-minute window, since seen tracks the certstream observation time.)

  How it executes — the EXPLAIN confirms the efficient path:
  AggregateExec  aggr=[min(…), max(…), avg(…)]
      SfstExec: projection=1 cols          ← time window pushed in here; only
  body.data.seen read
  No FilterExec — the timestamp range is pushed into SfstExec, so the index
  narrows to the in-window rows first, then min/max/avg compute over just the
  body.data.seen column of the survivors (projection=1 cols). So you get
  index-pruned time selection + a one-column scan for the math.

  You can drop in any window and any of the standard aggregates (sum, count,
  stddev, approx_percentile_cont, etc.) the same way — e.g.:
  SELECT date_bin(INTERVAL '30 seconds', timestamp) AS t,
         avg("body.data.seen") AS avg_seen, count(*) AS n
  FROM logs
  WHERE timestamp >= TIMESTAMP '2026-06-28T19:12:00'
  GROUP BY t ORDER BY t;
  That one even gets the GROUP BY date_bin pushed to the timeline bitmaps for
  the row counts, with the avg computed per bucket.

✻ Worked for 39s

❯ The single-file approach works like a charm. The question I have though, is   
  how should we use datafusion across all the SFST files the otel-plugin might  
  write?                                                                        

● Great question — this is where the experiment meets reality, and the honest
  framing is: the single-file SfstExec I built is the reusable leaf; the 
  multi-file problem is everything around it. Four things change going
  multi-file, and one of them is a genuine design fork that's yours to decide.

  What's actually different from single-file

  1. Which files to read (pruning) — you don't want to open every SFST for every
  query.
  2. One schema across many files — each SFST has its own field set; a
  DataFusion table has one schema. This is the hard fork.
  3. The live tail — un-sealed WAL data not yet in any SFST (the current sfsq
  folds it in).
  4. Remote IO — sealed files live in object storage (OpenDAL), not just local
  disk.

  The architecture that fits (and reuses what exists)

  The good news: sfsq already solves #1, #3, #4 — candidate selection by
  part_key + time window, SFST-wins-over-WAL dedup, tail folding,
  directory/remote resolution. So the DataFusion multi-file table should wrap 
  that selection logic, not reinvent it:

  - TableProvider::scan(filters) → ask the existing catalog/registry for
  candidate files (part_key + timestamp window come straight from the
  filename/catalog metadata — no file opens), exactly like sfsq does today.
  - Emit one partition per candidate file, each a per-file SfstExec (the leaf I
  already built) — plus optionally one tail partition scanning the un-sealed WAL
  for live parity.
  - DataFusion then parallelizes the partitions and merges/aggregates across
  them for free. Your filter/projection/facet pushdown happens per file.
  - Remote files: fetch bytes via OpenDAL in the async scan() (or stream
  lazily).

  So the leaf stays; you add a file-selecting, partition-fanning provider on top
  — structurally the same shape as sfsq::logs::run's fold, but expressed as
  DataFusion partitions.

  The real fork: one schema across files (your decision)

  Each stream has different fields, and a table needs one schema. Two models:

  (A) One table per stream (per part_key / service) — my recommendation.
  - Files of the same stream share a stable field set, so each table's schema is
  a bounded union of that stream's files — coherent and typed.
  - Multi-stream discovery via a DataFusion SchemaProvider/CatalogProvider that
  lists streams as tables; cross-stream queries are explicit UNION.
  - Pro: schemas stay sane and typed; pushdown (incl. facet/timeline) keeps
  working per stream. Con: many tables; cross-stream needs UNION.

  (B) One global logs table with a superset schema.
  - Either a giant union-of-all-fields schema (thousands of mostly-null columns)
  or a core + Map<Utf8,Utf8> hybrid (real typed columns for common fields, a
  dynamic map for the long tail).
  - Pro: single table, query everything. Con: schema is huge/null-heavy; the Map
  tail loses typed columns and pushdown; computing the schema means aggregating
  every file's META (needs a schema catalog).

  The schema-union problem is bounded per stream but explodes globally — which
  is exactly why I lean to (A).

  Pruning (the performance lever)

  - Partition pruning: supports_filters_pushdown on the stream/part_key column
  and timestamp → drop whole files using catalog metadata before opening
  anything. This is the biggest win and already-available data.
  - Value pruning (later): DataFusion's PruningStatistics::contained() backed by
  each file's FST → skip a file that can't contain WHERE service='x'. The recon
  flagged this as the natural FST hook.

  One strategic question to settle first

  DataFusion-across-all-files would be a second query stack alongside sfsq
  (which already does file-selection + merge + tail, and is what the agent
  response uses today). So decide why SQL:
  - If you want ad-hoc SQL / joins / arbitrary aggregation / a BI-style surface
  → DataFusion earns its place, and the design above is the path.
  - If the need is the existing log-query API
  (filter/facet/timeline/materialize) → sfsq already does multi-file and
  DataFusion is redundant there.

  Recommendation / suggested next experiment

  - Settle the why-SQL question and the (A) vs (B) schema fork first — those
  drive everything.
  - If you proceed, the low-risk next stage is: a per-stream multi-file 
  TableProvider over local sealed files, reusing sfsq's candidate-selection for
  pruning and my SfstExec as the per-file partition. Defer the tail, remote IO,
  and global schema to follow-ups.
  - That keeps each step verifiable against sfsq as an oracle (same files, same
  filters → same rows), just like I've been validating the single-file work.

  This is design-level, so it's your call on the two forks (why-SQL; per-stream
  vs global schema). Want me to write this up as a short design note (like the
  single-file one) and/or prototype the per-stream multi-file provider over
  local sealed files?

✻ Brewed for 1m 28s

※ recap: Goal: query SFST log files via DataFusion. The single-file integration is built, validated, and committed (scan, pushdown, facet/timeline aggregation, SQL CLI). Next: decide whether to extend to multi-file, which needs your call on two design forks (why SQL vs sfsq; per-stream vs global schema).
